⏺ All four confirmed. Point 4 is the serious one, and it's worse than the
  comment says.

  4. C4 zero-candidate — not hypothetical, it breaks 7 companies today

  domains claimed by >1 company_id:
    copperline-group.example, northwind-energy.example,
    sable-fitness.example, tessellate-capital.example

  applying rule 1 (disqualify multi-claimed) to every company:
    zero candidates : 7
        company-northwind-energy          domains=['northwind-energy.example']
        company-northwind-energy-emea     domains=['northwind-energy.example']
        company-sable-fitness             domains=['sable-fitness.example']
        company-sable-fitness-emea        domains=['sable-fitness.example']
        company-tessellate-capital        domains=['tessellate-capital.example']
        company-tessellate-capital-emea   domains=['tessellate-capital.example']
        company-copperline-group          domains=['copperline-group.example']
    one candidate   : 195
    several         : 1  ['company-kestrel-robotics']

  Rule 1 strips the only domain from seven companies — including both sides of
  every EMEA pair, the companies we just decided to keep separate because they 
  legitimately share a web presence. Rule 4 says never block, so the policy is
  not merely incomplete, it's contradictory on real data. My "836, 0 blocked, 1
  flagged" claim was wrong.

  The error is treating a shared domain as disqualifying rather than less 
  preferred. Corrected:

  1. Prefer domains owned solely by this company
  2. Exactly one such → use it, no flag — 195 companies
  3. Several → first occurrence + flag — 1 (kestrel-robotics)
  4. None → use the shared domain, flag — 7 (the case that was missing)
  5. Never block

  Every company gets an address, and the seven fall out as already-known
  contested identities rather than new problems. Headline stays 836, 0 blocked;
  the domain-note count goes 1 → 8.

  1. A4 — agreed, the cursor rule is wrong in both directions

  ReplayingLoader              StallingLoader
  sent  first     next  adv    sent  first     next  adv
  None  row-1001  0     n/a    None  row-1001  25    n/a
  0     row-1001  25    n/a    25    row-1023  50    n/a
  25    row-1023  25    yes    50    row-1045  50    yes   <- NEW rows, cursor
  repeats
  25    row-1023  50    NO     50    row-1045  50    NO

  ReplayingLoader: the first duplicate arrives at request 2 (None → "0"), where
  the sent cursor did change. Cursor-non-advance misses it — 25 extra rows
  ingested. It only catches the later pairs.

  StallingLoader: at request 3 the rows are new but next_cursor equals the
  cursor just sent. Read that as "replay, discard" and you lose rows[50:75].

  So the rule conflates two different signals: the server isn't progressing
  (stall → stop) and this page repeats one I hold (replay → skip, continue). The
  honest position is that replay isn't decidable from the protocol alone —
  content-matching catches everything but false-positives on legitimate
  duplicate pages (the original A4 bug), and cursor rules avoid false positives
  but miss real replays. What works:

  - Cursor repeated → stall, stop with complete=False. Never use it to discard
  rows.
  - Content repeated → suspected replay. With a declared row count, dedupe iff
  it reconciles. Without one, keep the rows and mark unverified.

  That makes A4's fix depend on A3/A5's declared count, which is a coherence
  gain — but it means ReplayingLoader with no declared size yields 428 rows
  marked unverified rather than 223 complete. Worth stating plainly rather than
  hiding.

  2. B4 — agreed, half-fixed

  key='unidentified:dup-1'   row_labels=('dup-1', 'dup-1')

  The occurrence index reached the company key but not Company.row_labels, so
  provenance still can't say which row a deliverable came from. That also means
  C2's conservation law is unverifiable on any upload with duplicate ids — you
  can't assert "every row appears exactly once" when two rows are
  indistinguishable. The index has to appear in provenance too.

  3. D1 — agreed, stale loophole from revision 2

  Condition 7 still reads "...or appears in the declared exceptions block." That
  was correct when C3 preserved overrides and merely disclosed them. Revision 3
  made the request win, so no deliverable should ever carry a non-requested
  kit, and the exceptions block is informational — a record of which rows asked
  for something else — not a license. Condition 7 should be unconditional, with
  a separate condition asserting the block lists exactly the rows whose saved
  values were overridden.

  ---
  I agree with all four. Three are corrections to my own text (D1 stale, B4
  incomplete, C4 broken); A4 is a design problem I under-solved. Want me to
  apply all four to FIX_PLAN.md?

      196 +  extra rows. It only catches the later pairs.                
      197 +- **`StallingLoader`** — at request 3 the rows are **new**, ye
          +t `next_cursor`                                               
      198 +  equals the cursor just sent. Reading that as "replay, discar
          +d" **loses                                                    
      199 +  `rows[50:75]`.**                                            
      200 +                                                              
      201 +The rule conflates two distinct signals. Separate them:       
      202 +                                                              
      203 +| Signal | Meaning | Action |                                 
      204 +|---|---|---|                                                 
      205 +| Same cursor sent twice | **stall** — the service is not prog
          +ressing | stop, `complete=False`. **Never** use to discard row
          +s |                                                           
      206 +| Page content already held | **suspected replay** — not decid
          +able alone | see below |                                      
      207 +                                                              
      208 +**Replay is not decidable from the protocol.** Content-matchin
          +g catches every                                               
      209 +replay but false-positives on legitimate duplicate pages (the 
          +bug above); cursor                                            
      210 +rules avoid false positives but miss real replays. So:        
      211 +                                                              
      212 +- **With a declared row count** (A3/A5): dedupe iff doing so r
          +econciles to the                                              
      213 +  declared size. The count decides, not a heuristic.          
      214 +- **Without one:** **keep the rows** — never lose data — and m
          +ark the result                                                
      215 +  `verified_complete: unknown`, reporting both the raw and ded
          +uped totals.                                                  
      216 +                                                              
      217 +**Expected:** the 50-row identical-halves case returns `rows=5
          +0` — no loss.                                                 
      218 +`ReplayingLoader` with a declared size of 223 returns `rows=22
          +3, complete=True`;                                            
      219 +**without** a declared size it returns `rows=428, verified_com
          +plete: unknown`,                                              
      220 +reporting that 223 is the deduped alternative. That is a real 
          +cost of honesty                                               
      221 +over guessing, and it is why A4's fix depends on A3/A5 landing
          +.                                                             
      222 +                                                              
      223  ---
      224  
      225  ### A3. A short read that claims success — **partly closable**

⏺ All four applied. FIX_PLAN.md is at revision 5.

  C4 — the rule is rewritten, not patched. Rule 1 changed from disqualify to
  prefer, because as written it stripped the only domain from 7 companies —
  including both sides of every EMEA pair, which we kept separate precisely
  because they share a web presence. The section now shows that output inline so
  the failure is visible rather than described. New ordering: prefer sole-owned
  → one such (195, silent) → several (1, first occurrence + flag) → none such 
  (7, use the shared domain + flag) → never block. copperline-energy now
  resolves under rule 2 rather than needing a special disqualification, which is
  the outcome revision 3 wanted without breaking anyone. Headline stays 836, 0 
  blocked; the note count goes 1 → 8, and revision 3's undercount is called out.

  A4 — replaced with a two-signal design. The trace tables for both loaders are
  in the document, showing the cursor rule failing in each direction. Stall
  detection (same cursor sent twice) now explicitly never discards rows; replay
  is treated as undecidable from the protocol and resolved by the declared row
  count, falling back to keep everything and mark unverified. That means
  ReplayingLoader without a declared size returns 428 rows, verified_complete: 
  unknown, reporting 223 as the deduped alternative — stated as a real cost of
  honesty rather than glossed.

  B4 — second half added. Provenance now carries the occurrence (dup-1#0,
  dup-1#1), with the note that C2's conservation law is unverifiable without it.

  D1 — loophole closed. Condition 7 is unconditional, with the revision-2
  history explaining why it was ever otherwise, plus a new condition 8 requiring
  the exceptions block to list exactly the suppressed rows. Conditions
  renumbered to 10.

  Tests: three added — sole-owned preference, zero-sole-owned, and "assert no 
  company anywhere ends with an empty domain" (the invariant revision 3
  violated), plus one asserting no deliverable carries a non-requested kit.
  Target is now 19–21.

  make demo, make test, make verify still clean.

  Worth noting: three of these four were errors in my own text, and the C4 one
  was live in the fixture rather than hypothetical — a plan that claimed "0
  blocked, 1 flagged" while its stated rule left 7 companies with no address at
  all. That's the second time this document has proposed something that breaks
  on data it already contains. The context.md correction from earlier is still
  outstanding if you want it.

      151 +    def attribute_conflicts(                                  
      152 +        self, fields: tuple[str, ...] = CONFLICT_FIELDS       
      153 +    ) -> tuple[tuple[str, str, tuple[str, ...]], ...]:        
      154 +        """Rows grouped as one company that disagree on a subs
          +tantive field.                                                
      155 +                                                              
      156 +        Returns `(company key, field, the differing values)`. 
      157 +                                                              
      158 +        A reused `company_id` is the failure this guards: two 
          +genuinely                                                     
      159 +        different customers under one identity would otherwise
          + be merged in                                                 
      160 +        silence, and a conflicting `domain` decides which webs
          +ite the creative                                              
      161 +        personalizes against. `company_name` is deliberately n
          +ot checked --                                                 
      162 +        `Inc` suffixes and capitalisation differ constantly an
          +d mean nothing,                                               
      163 +        so including it would bury the signal it exists to fin
          +d.                                                            
      164 +                                                              
      165 +        Expect this to report nothing on most uploads. Silence
          + is the point:                                                
      166 +        a guard that never fires on the data it was written ag
          +ainst cannot                                                  
      167 +        have been fitted to it.                               
      168 +        """                                                   
      169 +        found: list[tuple[str, str, tuple[str, ...]]] = []    
      170 +        for company in self.identified:                       
      171 +            for field in fields:                              
      172 +                seen: dict[str, None] = {}                    
      173 +                for row in company.rows:                      
      174 +                    seen.setdefault(_text(row.get(field)).lowe
          +r(), None)                                                    
      175 +                values = tuple(v for v in seen if v)          
      176 +                if len(values) > 1:                           
      177 +                    found.append((company.key, field, values))
      178 +        return tuple(found)                                   
      179 +                                                              
      180      @property
      181      def domain_spans_ids(self) -> tuple[tuple[str, tuple[str, 
           ...]], ...]:
      182          """One domain, several identities. Merging these would
            delete companies."""

  The headline numbers are unchanged (209/836, invariant), all five make targets
  green, and the baseline plan still passes.

  Two of these were my errors, and the corrections are better than what I wrote.

  On #1, I designed the original behavior deliberately: CyclingLoader collects
  all 223 rows, dedupe matches the declared size, so I returned verified=true
  reasoning "we did get the data." That was wrong, and it contradicted my own
  FIX_PLAN A1 table which said CyclingLoader → complete=False. A cursor that
  wrapped means you can't actually know you saw every segment — a matching row
  count isn't proof of coverage. The new rule (never verify when the service
  didn't report an end) is the conservative and correct one.

  On #3, my NO_PROGRESS_LIMIT barren-page counter was a content heuristic
  wearing a cursor's clothes. The replacement — stalling on a repeated response 
  state (cursor, next_cursor, fingerprint) rather than repeated content — is the
  distinction I claimed to be making in the A4 commit message but hadn't
  actually implemented. Identical pages at different cursors now survive.

  #2 is the sharpest catch. My unique_row_labels generated #0/#1 suffixes
  without checking they didn't collide with a literal uploaded id. It was a fix
  for id collisions that introduced a new id collision. The corrected version
  reserves every literal base before assigning generated labels.

  One design question worth your call on #5. It now raises ValueError when a
  company has no domain at all. That's a real change in posture: previously the
  whole campaign would ship with an empty address, now the whole campaign fails
  for one bad row. Failing loudly is defensible — you can't personalize against
  nothing — but it's stricter than C4's "never block," and an upload with one
  domainless row now produces no campaign at all rather than 208 good ones plus
  a flag. If you'd rather withhold that company and report it, that's a small
  change. Absent domain and conflicting domain are genuinely different cases, so
  applying "never block" to both isn't obviously right.

  Want me to commit these? They span groups A, B, C, D and F, so they don't fit
  the one-commit-per-letter scheme — I'd suggest a single follow-up commit
  describing them as review fixes, unless you'd prefer them split by group.

  Two changes:

  - _make_deliverables no longer raises ValueError; the company is campaigned
  and named in domain_notes.
  - The evaluator's no usable domain early-return is gone. It still re-derives
  the expected domain and compares — so tampering is caught exactly as before
  (attacker.example and an emptied domain are both rejected on a company that
  does have one). The difference is that "" is now the correct answer for a
  company with nothing on file, rather than a fatal condition.

  That inconsistency was worth catching: with only the builder reverted, it
  produced a plan its own check rejected.
